docs/findings.md.backup:| GEOMETRY-004 | 2025-11-23 | geometry, crystal, parameterization, ub-realign | Stage A incremental UB parameterization treats dxtbx crystal state (U₀, B₀) as authoritative and expresses orientation/cell as increments: `U(params) = ΔR(q_delta) @ U₀` (quaternion-based rotation), `B(params) = busing_levy_B_torch(a₀·exp(δlog_a), ..., α₀+Δα, ...)` (log-perturbations for lengths, delta-add for angles). Zero-point invariant: `q_delta=[1,0,0,0]` (identity), all deltas=0 → `U(0)=U₀`, `B(0)=B₀`, `A*(0)=U₀@B₀=A*_mapping`. One-way construction: `params → (U,B) → A*=U@B` (no A* decomposition in refinement loop). Helpers: `derive_orientation_from_quaternion_delta` (scipy quaternion-to-matrix + quaternion-to-Euler XYZ for nanobrag_torch API), `derive_B_from_cell_deltas` + `busing_levy_B_torch` (cctbx-based Busing-Levy B-matrix matching dxtbx lower-triangular convention). Validation: DB-AT-026 Tests 1-3 (zero-point ||U(0)-U₀||<1e-12, ||B(0)-B₀||<1e-12, ||A*(0)-A*_mapping||<1e-6), incremental UB convergence ≥0.2%, regression guard clean. Config flag: `use_incremental_ub=True` in `build_stage_a_lbfgs_closure`. Limitations: DB-AT-026 Test 4 (gradient flow) deferred — scipy/cctbx break PyTorch autograd; helpers are correct for forward passes and finite-difference validation, not suitable for direct gradient-based optimization (future: pytorch3d/kornia for differentiable ops). | dbex/nanobrag_bridge.py:1228-1407, dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:817-876,1036-1073, tests/dbex/test_ub_parameterization_roundtrip.py, plans/active/TORCH-GEOMETRY-UB-REALIGN-001/reports/ | Active |

docs/findings.md.backup:| GRADIENT-003 | 2025-11-23 | gradients, cpu-fallback, stage-b, hkl-grid, transfer-corruption | **ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED (95% confidence):** CPU fallback path produces zero Bragg intensities due to HKL grid CUDA→CPU `.to(device='cpu')` transfer corruption. HKL stats show nonsensical Miller indices on CPU (`k=[-1796,1708]` vs CUDA `k=[-14,14]`), causing 0% hit rate and all structure factors = default_F (gradient-free constant). The `.to()` device transfer preserves raw tensor data but corrupts grid's implicit Miller index mapping (grid coordinate → (h,k,l)). Minimal reproducer (loop i=220) proved CPU simulator works correctly (99% Bragg coverage) when HKL grid is built natively on CPU from scratch, not transferred. Parameter parity (loop i=219) ruled out dbex config mismatch (100% crystal/detector match CUDA vs CPU). Gradient tracking error ("element 0 of tensors does not require grad") is downstream symptom: 0% hit rate → all Bragg=0 → loss constant → no gradients flow to shell_modifiers. **Decision:** **CPU fallback support DEFERRED** - requires threading HKL source data (indices/amplitudes from MTZ) through to run_nanobrag_refinement API to enable native CPU grid reconstruction (Path A, MEDIUM complexity). CPU fallback NOT normative requirement; CUDA-only validation unblocks roadmap. **Future enhancement:** Implement Path A (HKL grid reconstruction from source on CPU) when API changes feasible. **Workaround:** Small detector (CUDA-only) test validates core Stage B shell modifier logic. | dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:547 (HKL grid `.to(device='cpu')` corruption site), plans/active/ARCH-REFINE-FLOW-001/reports/2025-11-23T{122329Z,140000Z}/{decision.md, root_cause_analysis_v4.md, pytest_stage_b_full_fixed.log} | Deferred |

docs/findings.md:| GEOMETRY-004 | 2025-11-23 | geometry, crystal, parameterization, ub-realign | Stage A incremental UB parameterization treats dxtbx crystal state (U₀, B₀) as authoritative and expresses orientation/cell as increments: `U(params) = ΔR(q_delta) @ U₀` (quaternion-based rotation), `B(params) = busing_levy_B_torch(a₀·exp(δlog_a), ..., α₀+Δα, ...)` (log-perturbations for lengths, delta-add for angles). Zero-point invariant: `q_delta=[1,0,0,0]` (identity), all deltas=0 → `U(0)=U₀`, `B(0)=B₀`, `A*(0)=U₀@B₀=A*_mapping`. One-way construction: `params → (U,B) → A*=U@B` (no A* decomposition in refinement loop). Helpers: `derive_orientation_from_quaternion_delta` (scipy quaternion-to-matrix + quaternion-to-Euler XYZ for nanobrag_torch API), `derive_B_from_cell_deltas` + `busing_levy_B_torch` (cctbx-based Busing-Levy B-matrix matching dxtbx lower-triangular convention). Validation: DB-AT-026 Tests 1-3 (zero-point **Consumers:** TORCH-GEOMETRY-SYNC-001.||U(0)-U₀||<1e-12, ||B(0)-B₀||<1e-12, ||A*(0)-A*_mapping||<1e-6), incremental UB convergence ≥0.2%, regression guard clean. Config flag: `use_incremental_ub=True` in `build_stage_a_lbfgs_closure`. Limitations: DB-AT-026 Test 4 (gradient flow) deferred — scipy/cctbx break PyTorch autograd; helpers are correct for forward passes and finite-difference validation, not suitable for direct gradient-based optimization (future: pytorch3d/kornia for differentiable ops). | dbex/nanobrag_bridge.py:1228-1407, dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:817-876,1036-1073, tests/dbex/test_ub_parameterization_roundtrip.py, plans/active/TORCH-GEOMETRY-UB-REALIGN-001/reports/ | Active |

docs/findings.md:| GRADIENT-003 | 2025-11-23 | gradients, cpu-fallback, stage-b, hkl-grid, transfer-corruption | **ROOT CAUSE IDENTIFIED (95% confidence):** CPU fallback path produces zero Bragg intensities due to HKL grid CUDA→CPU `.to(device='cpu')` transfer corruption. HKL stats show nonsensical Miller indices on CPU (`k=[-1796,1708]` vs CUDA `k=[-14,14]`), causing 0% hit rate and all structure factors = default_F (gradient-free constant). The `.to()` device transfer preserves raw tensor data but corrupts grid's implicit Miller index mapping (grid coordinate → (h,k,l)). Minimal reproducer (loop i=220) proved CPU simulator works correctly (99% Bragg coverage) when HKL grid is built natively on CPU from scratch, not transferred. Parameter parity (loop i=219) ruled out dbex config mismatch (100% crystal/detector match CUDA vs CPU). Gradient tracking error ("element 0 of tensors does not require grad") is downstream symptom: 0% hit rate → all Bragg=0 → loss constant → no gradients flow to shell_modifiers. **Decision:** **CPU fallback support DEFERRED** - requires threading HKL source data (indices/amplitudes from MTZ) through to run_nanobrag_refinement API to enable native CPU grid reconstruction (Path A, MEDIUM complexity). CPU fallback NOT normative requirement; CUDA-only validation unblocks roadmap. **Future enhancement:** Implement Path A (HKL grid reconstruction from source on CPU) when API changes feasible. **Workaround:** Small detector (CUDA-only) test validates core Stage B shell modifier logic. | dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:547 (HKL grid `.to(device='cpu')` corruption site), plans/active/ARCH-REFINE-FLOW-001/reports/2025-11-23T{122329Z,140000Z}/{decision.md, root_cause_analysis_v4.md, pytest_stage_b_full_fixed.log} | Deferred |

docs/fix_plan_archive.md:  * 2025-11-22T150000Z (diagnostic) — **Phase A Forward Model Discrepancy Diagnosed — B_ideal Computation Mismatch Bug Identified.** Diagnosed chi-squared discrepancy (990k vs 1.425B) between zero-point check and Adam loop step 0. Root cause confirmed via code inspection: `derive_u_matrix_from_mosflm_a_star` (dbex/nanobrag_bridge.py:844-862) computes B_ideal using `TorchCrystal.compute_cell_tensors()` while `_build_stage_a_components` (stage_a_mapping_adam_debug.py:323-326) computes B_ideal using `cctbx_cell().fractionalization_matrix().reshape(3,3).T`. Zero-point check succeeds because it uses `use_mapping_zero_geometry=True` (MOSFLM A* direct, bypasses U-matrix reconstruction), while Adam loop uses `use_mapping_zero_geometry=False` (forces U @ B reconstruction even at step 0). **First Divergence:** Chi-squared mismatch is NOT an optimizer issue (H1 REJECTED) or forward model numerical pathology (H3 REJECTED), but a B_ideal computation inconsistency bug. Fix required: Refactor `derive_u_matrix_from_mosflm_a_star` to return BOTH (U_matrix, B_ideal_reciprocal) and update both `stage_a_mapping_adam_debug.py` and `dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py` to use the returned B_ideal consistently (delete cctbx_cell() code). **Metrics:** zero_point_check chi²=989,645.5, Adam step 0 chi²=1,425,248,640 (1440× ratio). Subsequent steps try to recover but stuck in bad local minimum. **Hypothesis verdicts:** H1 PRIMARY (HIGH ~85%), H2 variance instability UNLIKELY (LOW ~10%, no oscillations, zero-point healthy), H3a gradient NaN/Inf REJECTED (0%, no NaN/Inf detected), H3b gradient explosion SYMPTOM (LOW ~15%, gradients large but stable/decreasing), H4 quaternion constraint NOT TESTABLE (A_scale_only has train_orientation=False). **Decision: Path A (Targeted Fix)** — Implement Adam LR reduction to 1e-5 for U-matrix parameterization (10× lower than current 1e-4). docs/fix_plan_archive.md:  * 2025-11-23T060500Z (Galph, review_or_housekeeping) — **Phase B1a-loop3 Blocker Review — One-Line Bugfix Identified.** Reviewed Ralph's Phase B1a-loop3 blocker (commit 3929b6d, 2025-11-23T060000Z artifacts). Ralph successfully extracted all three helpers (helper1 ~328 lines, helper2 ~717 lines, helper3 ~156 lines) and refactored run_nanobrag_refinement (reduced by 692 lines), BUT regression guard test_stage_a_expansion FAILED with `'NoneType' object has no attribute 'zero_grad'` error. **Root Cause Identified (HIGH confidence ~95%):** The `params` list is stored in helper1_result['params'] at top level of the returned dict (dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:1003) but NOT in the param_values dict. Helper2 (_build_stage_a_lbfgs_closure) receives param_values as parameter and tries to extract params via `params = param_values.get('params', [])` at line 1073, which returns empty list `[]` as default because the 'params' key is missing from param_values. The LBFGS optimizer then receives an empty params list and fails immediately when trying to call zero_grad() on the (non-existent) parameters. **Fix:** Add `'params': params,` to the param_values dict at line 943 in _build_stage_a_params helper (one-line change). **Evidence:** Pytest log shows `closure_evals: 0` confirming optimizer.step() failed before calling the closure, telemetry shows status='error' with message "'NoneType' object has no attribute 'zero_grad'", all param_deltas show delta=0.0 (no optimization occurred). **Verification:** The params list construction at lines 802-820 is CORRECT (filtered based on parameterization mode: default 8 params, U-matrix appends q_params, incremental UB replaces with 8 different params). No None values should be in the list. docs/fix_plan_archive.md:  * 2025-12-02T213235Z — Debug (Phase D.3 Batch 1 telemetry bugfix): Ralph's test migration (commit 7b0a016d) resulted in 5/5 tests FAILED with telemetry key mismatch (expected "A"/"B"/"C", got "stage_a"/"stage_b"/"stage_c"). Root cause: RefinementEngine.run() returns telemetry keyed by stage.name ("stage_a", "stage_b", "stage_c") but all test code expects legacy keys ("A", "B", "C"). Evidence: pytest log shows assertions like `assert 'A' in telemetry_dict` failing with actual keys `{'stage_a': ...}`. The facade (dbex/nanobrag_refinement.py:246, 428) explicitly converted to "A"/"B"/"C" for backward compatibility, and the CLI refactor (dbex/refine_one.py:593, 611, 616) also expects legacy keys (but wasn't caught because CLI tests were mocked). Issued corrective Do Now directing Ralph to add telemetry key mapping layer in engine.py::run() that converts "stage_a" → "A", "stage_b" → "B", "stage_c" → "C" before returning, following facade precedent exactly.
